to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
T
be a thick theory,
M
an e.c. model of
T
, and let
a,b,c
be tuples. Let also
q(x,y)
be a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(bc/M)
and write
r(x)=q|_{x}
. If
\Lstp(a/Mb)
does not
r
-Ls-divide then there is
c^{*}b\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}cb
such that
\Lstp(a/Mbc^{*})
does not
q
-Ls-divide.

Proof.

Let
(b_{i},c_{i})_{i<\lambda}
be a Morley sequence over
M
in
q
for some big enough
\lambda
. Since
(b_{i})_{i<\lambda}
is a Morley sequence over
M
in
r
and
\Lstp(a/Mb)
does not
r
-divide there is
a^{\prime}
with
a^{\prime}b_{0}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}ab
such that
(b_{i})_{i<\lambda}
is
Ma^{\prime}
-indiscernible.

Let
f\in\Aut_{f}(\mathfrak{M}/M)
be such that
f(a^{\prime}b_{0})=ab
and put
(b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})=(f(b_{i}),f(c_{i}))
. Then
b_{0}^{\prime}=b
,
(b_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\lambda}
is
Ma
-indiscernible and
(b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\lambda}
is a Morley sequence over
M
in
q
.

Let
M^{\prime}\supseteq Ma
be
\lambda_{T}
-saturated and use ?THM?
to find
M^{\prime}
-indiscernible
(b_{i}^{\prime\prime},c_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i<\lambda}
based on
(b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\lambda}
and such that
\operatorname{d}_{M}((b_{i}^{\prime\prime},c_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i<\lambda},(b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\lambda})\leq 1
. In particular
(b_{i}^{\prime\prime},c_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i<\lambda}
is a Morley sequence over
M
in
q
. Let
i<\lambda
be such that
b_{0}^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M^{\prime}}b_{i}^{\prime}
then
b_{0}^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Ma}b_{i}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Ma}b_{0}^{\prime}=b
. So there is
g\in\Aut_{f}(\mathfrak{M}/Ma)
such that
g(b_{0}^{\prime\prime})=b
. Set
c^{*}=g(c_{0}^{\prime\prime})
, so
bc^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{0}^{\prime\prime}c_{0}^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{0}^{\prime}c_{0}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{0}c_{0}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc
. Finally, since
(g(b_{i}^{\prime\prime}),g(c_{i}^{\prime\prime}))_{i<\lambda}
is a Morley sequence over
M
in
q
starting with
bc^{*}
that is
Ma
-indiscernible, we conclude that
\Lstp(a/Mbc^{*})
does not
q
-Ls-divide.
∎

Corollary 4.14

(Extension)
.

In a thick NSOP
1
theory we have that if
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
then for any
c
there is
c^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}c
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We first prove a weaker version where we conclude
c^{\prime}\equiv_{Mb}c
instead of
c^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}c
.

Let
q(x,y)
be an
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(bc/M)
and write
r(x)=q|_{x}
, where
x
matches
b
. Since
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
there is
a^{\prime}b\equiv_{M}ab
such that
\Lstp(a^{\prime}/Mb)
does not
r
-Ls-divide. By ?THM?
we thus find
bc^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc
such that
\Lstp(a^{\prime}/Mbc^{*})
does not
q
-Ls-divide. Letting
c^{\prime}
be such that
abc^{\prime}\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}bc^{*}
then satisfies
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
and furthermore we have
bc^{\prime}\equiv_{M}bc^{*}\equiv_{M}bc
.

Now we use the weaker version to prove the full version. Let
N\supseteq Mb
be some
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model. By the above we can find
N^{\prime}\equiv_{Mb}N
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}N^{\prime}
. Then using the above again we find
c^{\prime}\equiv_{N^{\prime}}c
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}N^{\prime}c^{\prime}
. Since
Mb\subseteq N^{\prime}
we thus get
c^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}c
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
, as required.
∎

5.
EM-modelling and parallel-Morley sequences

In this section we will introduce some tools which will be useful later in certain tree constructions.

Definition 5.1

(
[
KKS14
, Defintion 2.1]
)
.

The
Shelah language

L_{s}=\{\trianglelefteq,\wedge,<_{lex},(P_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\omega}\}

consists of binary relation symbols
\trianglelefteq,<_{lex}
, a binary function symbol
\wedge
, and unary relation symbols
P_{\alpha}
. We will consider a tree
\omega^{\leq k}
(with
k<\omega
) as an
L_{s}
-structure, where
\trianglelefteq
is interpreted as the containment relation,
<_{lex}
as the lexicographic order,
\wedge
as the meet function and
P_{\alpha}
as the
\alpha
-th level of the tree.

Definition 5.2

(
[
KKS14
, Definition 3.7]
)
.

Let
I
be an arbitrary index structure and
C
an arbitrary set of parameters. The
EM-type
of a tuple
A=(a_{i})_{i\in I}
over
C
is the partial type in variables
(x_{i})_{i\in I}
, consisting of all the formulas of the form
\varphi(x_{\bar{i}})
over
C
(where
\bar{i}
is a tuple in
I
) satisfying the following property:
\models\varphi(a_{\bar{j}})
holds whenever
\bar{j}
is a tuple in
I
with
\qftp_{I}(\bar{j})=\qftp_{I}(\bar{i})
. We let
EM_{I}(A/C)
denote this partial type.

In particular, we will write
EM_{s}(A/C)
[respectively,
EM_{<}(A/C)
] for
EM_{I}(A/C)
where
I
is considered as an
L_{s}
-structure [respectively, a
\{<\}
-structure].

Definition 5.3
.

Let
I
be an index structure and let
A=(a_{i})_{i\in I}
and
B=(b_{i})_{i\in I}
be
I
-indexed tuples of compatible parameters. We will say that
A
is
EM_{I}
-based on
B
over
C
if
EM_{I}(A/C)\supseteq EM_{I}(B/C)
.

Corollary 5.4
.

If
A
is any set of parameters, then for any compatible sequence
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
there is an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
which is
EM_{<}
-based on
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
over
A
.

Proof.

By compactness there is a sequence
(a^{\prime}_{i})_{i<\lambda_{|T|+|A|+|a_{0}|}}
which is
EM_{<}
-based on
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
over
A
.
Then by ?THM?
there is an
A
-indiscernible sequence
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}
which is
EM_{<}
-based on
(a^{\prime}_{i})_{i<\lambda_{|T|+|A|+|a_{0}|}}
over
A
, hence
EM_{<}
-based on
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
over
A
.
∎

In what follows we consider
\omega^{\leq k}
as an
L_{s}
-structure (see ?THM?
). We will only work with trees of width
\omega
, as we will only need those, but everything naturally works for arbitrary (infinite) widths.

Definition 5.5
.

We call a tree
(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}

s-indiscernible over
C
if for any
\bar{\eta},\bar{\nu}\subseteq\omega^{\leq k}
such that
\bar{\eta}\equiv_{qf}\bar{\nu}
we have that
a_{\bar{\eta}}\equiv_{C}a_{\bar{\nu}}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose
\bar{\eta}=(\eta_{0},\dots,\eta_{n-1})\equiv_{qf}\bar{\nu}=(\nu_{0},\dots,\nu_{n-1})
are tuples of elements of
\omega^{\leq k}
for some
k<\omega
.
Then there exists a sequence
I
of
n
-tuples of elements of
\omega^{\leq k}
such that
\bar{\eta}\smallfrown I
and
\bar{\nu}\smallfrown I
are qf-indiscernible sequences in
\omega^{\leq k}
.

Proof.

Let
l<\omega
be such that
\bar{\eta},\bar{\nu}\subseteq\{\emptyset\}\cup\{\xi\in\omega^{\leq k}\backslash\{\emptyset\}:\xi(0)<l\}
.
For every
0<m<\omega
choose a tuple
\bar{\chi}^{m}\subseteq\{\emptyset\}\cup\{\xi\in\omega^{\leq k}\backslash\{\emptyset\}:ml\leq\xi(0)<(m+1)l\}
such that
\bar{\chi}^{m}\equiv_{qf}\bar{\eta}\equiv_{qf}\bar{\nu}
(for example, for every
n^{\prime}<n
put
\chi^{m}_{n^{\prime}}(0)=\eta_{n^{\prime}}(0)+ml
and
\chi^{m}_{n^{\prime}}(i)=\eta_{n^{\prime}}(i)
for every
0<i\leq k
). Finally, put
I=(\bar{\chi}^{m})_{0<m<\omega}
.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

If
T
is thick then s-indiscernibility is type-definable, i.e. for every
k<\omega
and a tuple of variables
y
there is a partial type
\pi((x_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},y)
over
\emptyset
such that for all
D
with
|D|=|y|
:
((a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},D)\models\pi
if and only if
(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is s-indiscernible over
D
.

More specifically, we can take
\pi((x_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},y)
to be the partial type that expresses that for any
(\eta_{0},\dots,\eta_{n-1})\equiv_{qf}(\nu_{0},\dots,\nu_{n-1})
the Lascar distance of
(x_{\eta_{0}},\dots,x_{\eta_{n-1}})
and
(x_{\nu_{0}},\dots,x_{\nu_{n-1}})
over
y
is at most 2.

Proof.

Let
\pi
be as above and consider arbitrary
(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
and
D
. If
((a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},D)\models\pi
then
(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is indiscernible over
D
as being at Lascar distance at most 2 over
D
implies equality of types over
D
.

Conversely, if
((a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},D)
is s-indiscernible over
D
and
\bar{\eta}=(\eta_{0},\dots,\eta_{n-1})\equiv_{qf}\bar{\nu}=(\nu_{0},\dots,\nu_{n-1})
, then with
I=(\bar{\chi}^{m})_{0<m<\omega}
given by ?THM?
we have that
a_{\bar{\eta}}\smallfrown(a_{\bar{\chi}^{m}})_{0<m<\omega}
and
a_{\bar{\nu}}\smallfrown(a_{\bar{\chi}^{m}})_{0<m<\omega}
are both indiscernible sequences over
D
, so
a_{\bar{\eta}}
and
a_{\bar{\nu}}
are at Lascar distance at most 2 over
D
.
∎

We now adapt the proof of
[
KKS14
, Theorem 4.3]
to obtain the
EM_{s}
-modeling property for positive logic.

Proposition 5.8
.

Suppose
T
is thick and consider arbitrary set of parameters
D
and
k<\omega
. Then for any tree
A=(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
of compatible tuples there is an s-indiscernible over
D
tree
C=(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
which is
EM_{s}
-based on
A
over
D
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
k
. The case
k=0
is trivial. Suppose the assertion holds for some
k
and consider any
A=(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
. For any
i<\omega
consider an
\omega^{\leq k}
-indexed tree
A_{i}:=(a_{i\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
. Using the inductive hypothesis we choose inductively for each
i<\omega
a tree
B_{i}=(b^{i}_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
which is s-indiscernible over
Da_{\emptyset}B_{<i}A_{>i}
and
EM_{s}
-based on
A_{i}
over
Da_{\emptyset}B_{<i}A_{>i}
.
Let
B=(b_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
where
b_{\emptyset}=a_{\emptyset}
and
b_{i\smallfrown\xi}=b^{i}_{\xi}
for every
i<\omega
and
\xi\in\omega^{\leq k}
.

Claim 1
.

B_{i}
is s-indiscernible over
Db_{\emptyset}B_{\neq i}
for every
i<\omega
.

Fix
i<\omega
. We will show by induction on
j
that
B_{i}
is s-indiscernible over
Db_{\emptyset}B_{<i}B_{i+1}\dots B_{j-1}A_{\geq j}
for every
j>i
, which is enough by ?THM?
. For
j=i+1
this follows directly from the choice of
B_{i}
. Now suppose the assertion holds for some
j>i
.
By ?THM?
there is a type
\pi((x_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}},\bar{y})
over
D^{\prime}:=Db_{\emptyset}B_{<i}B_{i+1}\dots B_{j-1}A_{>j}
, where
\bar{y}=(y_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
, expressing that
(x_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is s-indiscernible over
D^{\prime}\bar{y}
. Then
B_{i}A_{j}\models\pi
. Note that the type
\pi(B_{i},\bar{y})
is invariant under all permutations of
\bar{y}
, hence if
\varphi(y_{\eta_{0}},\dots,y_{\eta_{n-1}})\in\pi(B_{i},\bar{y})
then
\varphi(y_{\nu_{0}},\dots,y_{\nu_{n-1}})\in\tp(A_{j}/D^{\prime}B_{i})
for all
\nu_{0},\dots,\nu_{n-1}\in\omega^{\leq k}
. In particular,
\pi(B_{i},\bar{y})\subseteq EM_{s}(A_{j}/D^{\prime}B_{i})
. Thus, by the choice of
B_{j}
, we have that
\pi(B_{i},\bar{y})\subseteq EM_{s}(B_{j}/D^{\prime}B_{i})
, so in particular
B_{i}B_{j}\models\pi
. Hence
B_{i}
is indiscernible over
D^{\prime}B_{j}=Db_{\emptyset}B_{<i}B_{i+1}\dots B_{j}A_{\geq j+1}
, as required.

Claim 2
.

B
is
EM_{s}
-based on
A
over
D
.

Consider any
i<\omega
and the trees
E=(e_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
and
F=(f_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
given by
e_{\emptyset}=f_{\emptyset}=a_{\emptyset}
,
e_{j\smallfrown\eta}=\begin{cases}b_{j\smallfrown\eta}\mbox{ for }j<i\\
a_{j\smallfrown\eta}\mbox{ for }j\geq i\end{cases}
, and
f_{j\smallfrown\eta}=\begin{cases}b_{j\smallfrown\eta}\mbox{ for }j\leq i\\
a_{j\smallfrown\eta}\mbox{ for }j>i\end{cases}
.
We will prove that
\pi_{0}:=EM_{s}(E/D)\subseteq EM_{s}(F/D)=:\pi_{1}

which clearly is sufficient to prove the claim.
Let
\bar{x}=(x_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
be a tuple of variables compatible with
a_{\eta}
’s. We naturally view
\pi_{0}
and
\pi_{1}
as partial types in the variable
\bar{x}
. Consider any formula
\varphi(x_{\eta_{0}},\dots,x_{\eta_{l}},x_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,x_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})\in\pi_{0}
over
D
with
\eta_{0},\dots,\eta_{l}\in K_{i}:=\{i\smallfrown\xi:\xi\in\omega^{\leq k}\}
and
\eta_{l+1},\dots,\eta_{l^{\prime}}\in\omega^{\leq k+1}\backslash K_{i}
. We will be done if we show that
\models\varphi(f_{\eta_{0}},\dots,f_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})
.
Write
\eta_{t}=i\frown\xi_{t}
for
t=0,1,\dots,l
. For any
\xi^{\prime}_{0},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{l}\in\omega^{\leq k}
with
\qftp_{L_{s}}(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\dots,\xi^{\prime}_{l})=\qftp_{L_{s}}(\xi_{0},\dots,\xi_{l})
we have
\qftp_{L_{s}}(\eta_{0},\dots,\eta_{l^{\prime}})=\qftp_{L_{s}}(i\smallfrown\xi_{0},\dots,i\smallfrown\xi_{l},\eta_{l+1},\dots,\eta_{l^{\prime}})=\qftp_{L_{s}}(i\smallfrown\xi^{\prime}_{0},\dots,i\smallfrown\xi^{\prime}_{l},\eta_{l+1},\dots,\eta_{l^{\prime}})
, hence, as
\varphi\in\pi_{0}
, we get that
\models\varphi(e_{i\smallfrown\xi^{\prime}_{0}},\dots,e_{i\smallfrown\xi^{\prime}_{l}},e_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,e_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})
. This shows that
\varphi(y_{\xi_{0}},\dots,y_{\xi_{l}},e_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,e_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})\in EM_{s}(A_{i}/a_{\emptyset}A_{<i}B_{>i})
where
A_{i}
is naturally indexed by
\omega^{\leq k}
, so, by the choice of
B_{i}
we get that
\models\varphi(b^{i}_{\xi_{0}},\dots,b^{i}_{\xi_{l}},e_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,e_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})
. As
(b^{i}_{\xi_{0}},\dots,b^{i}_{\xi_{l}},e_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,e_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})=(f_{i\smallfrown\xi_{0}},\dots,f_{i\smallfrown\xi_{l}},f_{\eta_{l+1}},\dots,f_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})=(f_{\eta_{0}},\dots,f_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})
, this means that
\models\varphi(f_{\eta_{0}},\dots,f_{\eta_{l^{\prime}}})
, as required.

By ?THM?
we find a sequence
(C_{i})_{i<\omega}=((c^{i}_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}})_{i<\omega}
which is
EM_{<}
-based on
(B_{i})_{i<\omega}
over
Db_{\emptyset}
and indiscernible over
Db_{\emptyset}
. Let
C=(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
be given by
c_{\emptyset}=b_{\emptyset}
and
c_{i\smallfrown\xi}=c^{i}_{\xi}
for any
\xi\in\omega^{\leq k}
and
i<\omega
. By ?THM?
and ?THM?
we get that
C_{i}
is s-indiscernible over
C_{\neq i}Dc_{\emptyset}
for every
i<\omega
, which, together with
Dc_{\emptyset}
-indiscernibility of
(C_{i})_{i<\omega}
easily gives that
C
is s-indiscernible over
D
(as in
[
KKS14
]
). It is left to prove:

Claim 3
.

C
is
EM_{s}
-based on
B
(and hence on
A
) over
D
.

Consider any formula
\varphi(x_{i_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l}},x_{\emptyset})\in EM_{s}(B/D)
with
i_{1},\dots,i_{l}\in\omega
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{l}\in\omega^{\leq k}
. Then for every
j_{1},\dots,j_{l}\in\omega
with
\qftp_{\{<\}}(j_{1},\dots,j_{l})=\qftp_{\{<\}}(i_{1},\dots,i_{l})
we have that
\qftp_{L_{s}}(j_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1},\dots,j_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l},\emptyset)=\qftp_{L_{s}}(i_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1},\dots,i_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l},\emptyset)
, so
\models\varphi(b_{j_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1}},\dots,b_{j_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l}},b_{\emptyset})
. This means that

\varphi(x_{i_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l}},b_{\emptyset})\in EM_{<}((B_{i})_{i<\omega}/b_{\emptyset}D),

hence by the choice of
C
we have
\models\varphi(c_{i_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1}},\dots,c_{i_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l}},c_{\emptyset})
so
\varphi(x_{i_{1}\smallfrown\xi_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{l}\smallfrown\xi_{l}},x_{\emptyset})\in EM_{s}(C/D)
, as required.
∎

Definition 5.9
.

Let
I
be a linearly ordered set. For a global
M
-
Ls
-invariant type
q
, we will call a sequence
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
a
parallel-Morley sequence in
q
over
M
, if there is some
(b_{i})_{i\in I}\models q^{\otimes I}|_{M}
such that the pair
(a_{i},b_{i})
starts an
Ma_{>i}b_{>i}
-indiscernible sequence for every
i\in I
. We will say that
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
is a parallel-Morley sequence in
\tp(a/M)
if it is a parallel-Morley sequence in some global
M
-Ls-invariant type
q\supseteq\tp(a/M)
.

In the semi-Hausdorff case we can replace the condition “
(a_{i},b_{i})
starts an
Ma_{>i}b_{>i}
-indiscernible sequence” by “
a_{i}\equiv_{Ma_{>i}b_{>i}}b_{i}
”. The reason for which we need the stronger condition in thick theories is that equality of types is not necessarily type-definable there, so some of the compactness arguments below would not work with the weaker condition.

Note that a parallel-Morley sequence is
not
required to be indiscernible. The reason for the name “parallel-Morley sequence” is because such a sequence is parallel to a Morley sequence, in the sense of the parallel sequences lemma (?THM?
). We make this precise in ?THM?
, for which we first slightly reformulate the parallel sequences lemma.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
T
be thick and suppose
\varphi(x,y)
is a formula and
(c_{i,0},c_{i,1})_{i\in I}
is an infinite sequence of pairs with
(c_{i,1})_{i\in I}
indiscernible, such that:

(i)

for every
i\in I
, the pair
(c_{i,0},c_{i,1})
starts a
c_{>i,0}c_{>i,1}
-indiscernible sequence;

(ii)

\{\varphi(x;c_{i,0}):i\in I\}
is consistent;

(iii)

\{\varphi(x;c_{i,1}):i\in I\}
is inconsistent.

Then
T
has SOP
1
.

Proof.

We may assume the tuples
c_{i,0}
and
c_{i,1}
to be finite. As
(c_{i,1})_{i\in I}
is indiscernible and
\{\varphi(x,c_{i,1}):i\in I\}
is inconsistent, there is some
\psi(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k})
that implies
\neg\exists x(\varphi(x,y_{1})\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi(x,y_{k}))
such that for any
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}\in I
we have
\models\psi(c_{i_{1},1},\ldots,c_{i_{k},1})
. Call this
\psi
-inconsistent. By compactness there is a sequence of pairs
(\bar{c^{\prime}}_{i})_{i<\lambda_{T}}=(c^{\prime}_{i,0},c^{\prime}_{i,1})_{i<\lambda_{T}}
such that
(c^{\prime}_{i,0},c^{\prime}_{i,1})
starts a
\bar{c^{\prime}}_{>i}
-indiscernible sequence for every
i<\lambda_{T}
,
\{\varphi(x,c^{\prime}_{i,0}):i<\lambda_{T}\}
is consistent and
\{\varphi(x,c^{\prime}_{i,1}):i<\lambda_{T}\}
is
\psi
-inconsistent. Then an indiscernible sequence based on
(\bar{c^{\prime}}_{i})_{i<\lambda_{T}}
will satisfy the assumptions of ?THM?
, so
T
has SOP
1
.
∎

By Kim’s Lemma (?THM?
) and ?THM?
we easily get the following.

Corollary 5.11
.

Suppose
T
is thick NSOP
1
with an e.c. model
M
,
\Sigma(x,b)
is a partial type,
I
is an infinite linearly ordered set, and
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
a parallel-Morley sequence in
\tp(b/M)
. If
\bigcup\{\Sigma(x,b_{i}):i\in I\}
is consistent then
\Sigma(x,b)
does not Kim-divide over
M
. If
(b_{i})_{i\in I}
is indiscernible over
M
, then the converse also holds.

Definition 5.12
.

Let
M
be an e.c. model and
q
a global
M
-Ls-invariant type.

(i)

We will say that a tree
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is
q
-spread-out over
M
if for any
\eta_{1}\in\omega^{1},\eta_{2}\in\omega^{2},\dots,\eta_{k}\in\omega^{k}
such that
\eta_{1}>_{lex}\eta_{2}>_{lex}\dots>_{lex}\eta_{k}
and
(\forall l<l^{\prime}\leq k)(\eta_{l^{\prime}}\wedge\eta_{l}\in\omega^{l-1})
we have that
(c_{\eta_{k}},\dots,c_{\eta_{1}})
is a Morley sequence in
q
over
M
.

(ii)

We will say that
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is
weakly
q
-spread-out over
M
if
(c_{\eta_{k}},\dots,c_{\eta_{1}})\models q^{\otimes k}|_{M}
for
\eta_{i}
’s as in (i).

Lemma 5.13
.

Let

((c_{i\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}})_{i<\omega}

for any choice of root

.

.

Proof.

(i) Let
\eta_{k}\in\omega^{k},\ldots,\eta_{1}\in\omega^{1}
be such that
\eta_{1}>_{lex}\ldots>_{lex}\eta_{k}
and
(\forall l<l^{\prime}\leq k)(\eta_{l^{\prime}}\wedge\eta_{l}\in\omega^{l-1})
. We will prove that
(c_{\eta_{k}},\ldots,c_{\eta_{1}})
is a Morley sequence in
q
. For each
\ell\geq 2
let
\beta_{\ell}\in\omega^{1}

\eta_{\ell}\trianglerighteq\beta_{\ell}
. For every

\eta_{2}\wedge\eta_{\ell}=\eta_{2}|_{1}=\beta_{2}
, hence
\beta_{\ell}=\beta_{2}=:\beta
(and

\eta_{1}>_{{\textup{lex}}}\eta_{2}
). In particular
(c_{\eta_{k}},\ldots,c_{\eta_{2}})

M
as
(c_{0\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}}
. So, as
(c_{0\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}}
is
q
-spread-out by assumption,
(c_{\eta_{k}},\ldots,c_{\eta_{2}})
is a Morley sequence in
q
. As
((c_{i\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}})_{i<\omega}
is a Morley sequence in
r
, we have that
(c_{\eta_{1}\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}}
, which contains
c_{\eta_{1}}
, has the same Lascar strong type over
M(c_{\beta\smallfrown\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k-1}}
, which contains
Mc_{\eta_{k}}\ldots c_{\eta_{2}}
, as some realisation of
r
. Since
q(x)=r|_{x}
we see that
c_{\eta_{1}}
has the same Lascar strong type over
Mc_{\eta_{k}},\ldots,c_{\eta_{2}}
as some realisation of
q
. So we conclude that
(c_{\eta_{k}},\ldots,c_{\eta_{1}})
is indeed a Morley sequence in
q
.

(ii) This holds because the condition on
(\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{k})
in the definition of weak
q
-spread-outness is expressible by a quantifier-free
L_{s}
-formula.

(iii) Put
a^{\prime}_{i}:=c_{0^{k-i-1}\smallfrown 1}
for
i<k
. Then
(a^{\prime}_{i})_{i<k}\models q^{\otimes k}|_{M}
by weak
q
-spread-outness, and
(a_{i},a^{\prime}_{i})
starts an
M_{a_{>i}a^{\prime}_{>i}}
-indiscernible sequence for each
i<k
by
s
-indiscernibility.
∎

6.
Symmetry

Lemma 6.1

(Chain condition)
.

Let
T
be a thick NSOP
1
theory and let
M
be an e.c. model. Let
(b_{i})_{i<\kappa}
be a Morley sequence in some global
M
-Ls-invariant
q(x)
. If
(b_{i})_{i<\kappa}
is
Ma
-indiscernible then
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}(b_{i})_{i<\kappa}
.

Proof.

We will prove that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{i_{1}}\ldots b_{i_{k}}
for all
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}<\kappa
. This is indeed enough by finite character. By
Ma
-indiscernibility of
(b_{i})_{i<\kappa}
we may assume
\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}=\{0,\ldots,k-1\}
.

We have
(b_{i})_{i<\omega}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}(\beta_{i})_{i<\omega}
for some
(\beta_{i})_{i<\omega}\models q^{\otimes\omega}
. Define the tuple
\gamma_{i}=(\beta_{ik},\beta_{ik+1},\ldots,\beta_{ik+k-1})
for all
i<\omega
. Then
(\gamma_{i})_{i<\omega}\models(q^{\otimes k})^{\otimes\omega}
by associativity of tensoring (?THM?
). We let
c_{i}=(b_{ik},b_{ik+1},\ldots,b_{ik+k-1})
for all
i<\omega
. Then
(c_{i})_{i<\omega}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}(\gamma_{i})_{i<\omega}
. So
(c_{i})_{i<\omega}
is a Morley sequence in
q^{\otimes k}
over
M
and
(c_{i})_{i<\omega}
is
Ma
-indiscernible. So
\tp(a/Mc_{0})=\tp(a/Mb_{0}\ldots b_{k-1})
does not
q^{\otimes k}
-divide, and thus
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{0}\ldots b_{k-1}
as required.
∎

Definition 6.2
.

Suppose
M
is an e.c. model,
q
a global type extending
\Lstp(a/M)
and
\lambda
a cardinal. We will say that the extension
q\supseteq\Lstp(a/M)
satisfies
(*)_{\lambda}
if for every
c
with
|c|\leq\lambda
there is a global
M
-Ls-invariant type
r(x,y)\supseteq\Lstp(ac/M)
extending
q(x)
(in particular,
q
is
M
-Ls-invariant).

Lemma 6.3
.

For any e.c. model
M
, tuple
a
and cardinal
\lambda
there is
q\supseteq\Lstp(a/M)
satisfying
(*)_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Let
M
,
a
and
\lambda
be as in the statement. Choose a small tuple
d
such that for any
c
with
|c|\leq\lambda
there is some
d^{\prime}\subseteq d
with
\Lstp(ad^{\prime}/M)=\Lstp(ac/M)
(this is possible as the number of Lascar types of tuples of fixed length over
M
is bounded by ?THM?
). Now take a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension
r(x,y)
of
\Lstp(ad/M)
, where
x
corresponds to
a
. Then
q:=r|_{x}
is an extension of
\Lstp(a/M)
satisfying
(*)_{\lambda}
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

If
q\supseteq\Lstp(a/M)
is finitely satisfiable in
M
then it satisfies
(*)_{\lambda}
for any cardinal
\lambda
(
[
Men20
, Lemma 3.4]
). However, finitely satisfiable extensions may not exist in thick theories.

Theorem 6.5

(Symmetry)
.

In a thick NSOP
1
theory
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
implies
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}a
.

Proof.

We may assume that
b
enumerates a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model containing
M
. If this is not the case let
N\supseteq Mb
be a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model. By extension, ?THM?
, we find
N^{\prime}\equiv_{Mb}N
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}N^{\prime}
. Now we replace
b
by
N^{\prime}
and we continue the proof.

Set
\lambda=|ab|
. By ?THM?
we can choose a global extension
q\supseteq\Lstp(a/M)
satisfying
(*)_{\lambda}
. Let
p(y,a)=\tp(b/Ma)
. We will show that there is a parallel-Morley sequence
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
in
q
over
M
such that
\bigcup_{i<\omega}p(y,a_{i})
is consistent, which is enough by ?THM?
. All the properties we wish
(a_{i})_{i<\omega}
to have are type-definable. It is thus enough to find such a sequence of length
k
for every
k<\omega
.

So fix any
k<\omega
. By backward induction on
k^{\prime}=k+1,k,\ldots,1
we will define trees
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}
where
S_{k^{\prime}}=\{\xi\in\omega^{\leq k+1}:0^{k^{\prime}-1}\trianglelefteq\xi\}
. We will write
S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}
for
S_{k^{\prime}}
without the root, so
S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}=S_{k^{\prime}}-\{0^{k^{\prime}-1}\}
. For each
k^{\prime}
the tree
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}
will satisfy the following conditions:

(A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}}

c_{\eta}c_{\nu}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}ab
for all
\nu\triangleright\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}
with
\nu\in{\omega}^{k+1}
and
\eta\in{\omega}^{\leq k}
;

(A2)
{}_{k^{\prime}}

(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}\cap\omega^{\leq k}}
is
q
-spread-out over
M
;

(A3)
{}_{k^{\prime}}

(the root is independent from the rest) we have
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}
.

For
k^{\prime}=k+1
we let
t
be a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(b/M)
. Since
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
we have that
\tp(a/Mb)
does not
t
-divide. By ?THM?
and our assumption on
b
this means that
\Lstp(a/Mb)
does not
t
-Ls-divide. So we find an
Ma
-indiscernible Morley sequence
(c_{0^{k}\smallfrown\alpha})_{\alpha<\omega}
in
t
with
c_{0^{k+1}}=b
. By ?THM?
we have that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}(c_{0^{k}\smallfrown\alpha})_{\alpha<\omega}
. So we pick
c_{0^{k}}=a
and directly satisfy (A3)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
. Condition (A2)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
is vacuous and (A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
follows directly from
Ma
-indiscernibility of
(c_{0^{k}\smallfrown\alpha})_{\alpha<\omega}
and the fact that
c_{0^{k+1}}=b
.

For the inductive step, suppose that we have constructed
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}
. By (A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
there is a tuple
d
such that
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}ad
. So, by
(*)_{\lambda}
there is a global
M
-Ls-invariant type
r(x,z)\supseteq q(x)
extending
\Lstp(c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}/M)
. By (A3)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
we have that
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}
. So since
b\subseteq(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}
and using our assumption on
b
we have by ?THM?
that
\Lstp(c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}/M(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}})
does not
r|_{z}
-Ls-divide. By extension for Ls-dividing, ?THM?
, we find
c
such that
c(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}
and
\Lstp(c/M(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}})
does not
r
-Ls-divide. So there is an
Mc
-indiscernible Morley sequence
((d_{\eta,i})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}})_{i<\omega}
in
r
such that
(d_{\eta,0})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}=(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}
. We set
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-2}}=c
and
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-2}\smallfrown i\smallfrown\zeta}=d_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}\smallfrown\zeta,i}
. Again, using ?THM?
we directly get (A3)
{}_{k^{\prime}-1}
.

Now (A2)
{}_{k^{\prime}-1}
follows from ?THM?
(i). We verify (A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}-1}
. Everything above the root consists of copies (via a Lascar strong automorphism over

) of
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}}
, so we only need to check that

for all
\nu\in S_{k^{\prime}-1}\cap\omega^{k+1}
. By indiscernibility we may assume

. Then (A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}-1}
follows from (A1)
{}_{k^{\prime}}
and the fact that
c_{0^{k^{\prime}-2}}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c_{0^{k^{\prime}-1}}(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{k^{\prime}}^{*}}
.

Thus the inductive step, and hence the construction of the tree
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{k+1}}=(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in S_{1}}
, is completed.

Consider the following condition:

(A1’)
1

c_{\eta}c_{\nu}\equiv_{M}ab
for all
\nu\triangleright\eta
with
\nu\in{\omega}^{k+1}
and
\eta\in{\omega}^{\leq k}
;

which is clearly implied by (A1)

as it is seen by the

-type of
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
over

. Letting
(c_{\eta}^{\prime})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
be an

-indiscernible tree which is

-based on
(c_{\eta})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
over

, we get that
(c_{\eta}^{\prime})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k+1}}
satisfies (A1’)

, and
(c_{\eta}^{\prime})_{\eta\in\omega^{\leq k}}
is weakly
