-spread-out over
M
by ?THM?
(ii).

Put
a_{i}=c_{0^{k+1-i}}^{\prime}
. Then
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})
is a parallel-Morley sequence in
q
over
M
by ?THM?
(iii), and by (A1’)
1
we have that
\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq k}p(y,a_{i})
is consistent because it is realised by
c_{0^{k+1}}^{\prime}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
T
be a thick theory. Suppose that
\varphi(x,y)
has SOP
1
, witnessed by
\psi(y_{1},y_{2})
. Then there is an e.c. model
M
and
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
,
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
such that
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}c_{2}
,
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{1}
,
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{2}
and
b_{1}c_{1}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{2}c_{2}
and
\models\varphi(b_{1},c_{1})\wedge\varphi(b_{2},c_{2})\wedge\psi(c_{1},c_{2})
.

Proof.

The proof is mostly the same as
[
HK21
, Proposition A.7]
but we have to adjust a few things throughout to get equality of Lascar strong types rather than just equality of types. As in that proof, we will use a Skolemisation technique for positive logic
[
HK21
, Lemma A.6]
. In such a Skolemised theory the positively definable closure of any set is an e.c. model and the reduct of an e.c. model (to the original language) is an e.c. model (of the original theory). It is not directly clear whether this Skolemisation construction preserves thickness, but that is not a problem. Ultimately we are interested in Lascar strong types in our original theory. So even though we technically work in a Skolemised theory the (type-definable) predicate
\operatorname{d}(x,y)\leq 1
should be taken as in our original theory.

Let
\kappa
be any cardinal. By compactness we find parameters
(a_{\eta})_{\eta\in 2^{<\kappa}}
such that:

(i)

for every
\sigma\in 2^{\kappa}
the set
\{\varphi(x,a_{\sigma|i}):i<\kappa\}
is consistent,

(ii)

for every
\eta,\nu\in 2^{<\kappa}
such that
\eta^{\frown}0\preceq\nu
we have
\models\psi(a_{\eta^{\frown}1},a_{\nu})
.

For a big enough cardinal
\lambda
, we construct by induction a sequence
(\eta_{i},\nu_{i})_{i<\lambda}
with
\eta_{i},\nu_{i}\in 2^{<\kappa}
such that:

(1)

\eta_{i}\trianglelefteq\eta_{j}
and
\eta_{i}\trianglelefteq\nu_{j}
for all
i<j<\lambda
;

(2)

\eta_{i}\trianglerighteq(\eta_{i}\wedge\nu_{i})\frown 0
,
\nu_{i}=(\eta_{i}\wedge\nu_{i})\frown 1
, and
(a_{\eta_{i}},a_{\nu_{i}})
starts an
a_{\eta_{<i}}a_{\nu_{<i}}
-indiscernible sequence for every
i<\lambda
.

Assume
(\eta_{j},\nu_{j})_{j<i}
has been constructed and set
\eta=\bigcup_{j<i}\eta_{j}
. If we chose
\kappa
to be large enough then, by applying ?THM?
to
(a_{\eta^{\frown}0^{\alpha\frown}1})_{\alpha>0}
, it follows that there are
0<\alpha<\beta<\kappa
such that
(a_{\eta^{\frown}0^{\alpha\frown}1},a_{\eta^{\frown}0^{\beta\frown}1})
starts an
{\{\eta_{j},\nu_{j}:j<i\}}
-indiscernible sequence. We set
\nu_{i}=\eta^{\frown}0^{\alpha\frown}1
and
\eta_{i}=\eta^{\frown}0^{\beta\frown}1
.

By (i) and (1), there is
b_{2}
realising
\{\varphi(x,a_{\eta_{i}}):i<\lambda\}
. Now let
(e_{i},d_{i})_{i<\omega+2}
be indiscernible over
b_{2}
based on
(a_{\eta_{i}},a_{\nu_{i}})_{i<\lambda}
.

Let
M
be the positively definable closure of
\{e_{i},d_{i}:i<\omega\}
. As discussed, we may assume
M
to be an e.c. model. Set
c_{1}=d_{\omega}
and
c_{2}=e_{\omega+1}
. Then
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{\{e_{i},d_{i}:i<\omega\}}^{u}c_{2}
and
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{\{e_{i},d_{i}:i<\omega\}}^{u}b_{2}
by indiscernibility. So
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}c_{2}
,
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{2}
and
\models\varphi(b_{2},c_{2})
. By construction
c_{1}c_{2}=d_{\omega}e_{\omega+1}\equiv a_{\nu_{i_{0}}}a_{\eta_{i_{1}}}
for some
i_{0}<i_{1}<\lambda
and thus
\models\psi(c_{1},c_{2})
by (ii), (1), and (2).

To find
b_{1}
we first claim that
\operatorname{d}_{M}(e_{\omega},d_{\omega})\leq 1
. By compactness it suffices to prove that
\operatorname{d}_{A}(e_{\omega},d_{\omega})\leq 1
for all finite
A\subseteq M
. By how we constructed
M
it then suffices to prove that
(e_{\omega},d_{\omega})
starts an indiscernible sequence over
\{e_{i},d_{i}:i<n\}
for all
n<\omega
. To prove this last statement we let
i_{0}<\ldots<i_{n+1}<\lambda
be such that

e_{0}d_{0}\ldots e_{n}d_{n}e_{\omega}d_{\omega}\equiv a_{\eta_{i_{0}}}a_{\nu_{i_{0}}}\ldots a_{\eta_{i_{n}}}a_{\nu_{i_{n}}}a_{\eta_{i_{n+1}}}a_{\nu_{i_{n+1}}}.

By how we constructed
(\eta_{i},\nu_{i})_{i<\lambda}
we have
(a_{\eta_{i_{n+1}}},a_{\nu_{i_{n+1}}})
starts an indiscernible sequence over
\{a_{\eta_{i_{0}}}a_{\nu_{i_{0}}}\ldots a_{\eta_{i_{n}}}a_{\nu_{i_{n}}}\}
. So the claim follows after applying the automorphism.

Now we leave the Skolemised theory and work in the original theory, so
\operatorname{d}(x,y)\leq 1
corresponds to actually having Lascar distance one. We have
c_{2}=e_{\omega+1}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}e_{\omega}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}d_{\omega}=c_{1}
, so there is
f\in\Aut_{f}(\mathfrak{M}/M)
such that
f(c_{2})=c_{1}
. Let
b_{1}=f(b_{2})
. Then
c_{2}b_{2}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c_{1}b_{1}
, hence also
\models\varphi(b_{1},c_{1})
and
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{1}
, as required.
∎

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
T
be a thick theory. The following are equivalent:

(i)

T
is NSOP
1
;

(ii)

(symmetry)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
implies
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}a
;

(iii)

(weak symmetry)
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{iLs}b
implies
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}a
.

Proof.

?THM?
is precisely (i)
\implies
(ii). For (ii)
\implies
(iii) we just note that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{iLs}b
implies
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
. Finally, for (iii)
\implies
(i) we proceed is as in
[
KR20
, Proposition 3.22]
replacing their reference to
[
CR16
]
by ?THM?
and being careful about using global Ls-invariant types instead of just global invariant types.

We prove the contrapositive, so assume
T
has SOP
1
. Then by ?THM?
there is an e.c. model
M
and
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
,
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
such that
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}c_{2}
,
c_{1}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{1}
,
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{2}
and
b_{1}c_{1}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{2}c_{2}
. Furthermore, for
p(x,c_{1})=\tp(b_{1}c_{1}/M)
, we have that
p(x,c_{1})\cup p(x,c_{2})
is inconsistent. In particular we have that
\Lstp(c_{1}/Mc_{2})
extends to a global
M
-Ls-invariant
q
. Then as
c_{1}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c_{2}
there is a Morley sequence
(d_{i})_{i<\omega}
in
q
with
d_{0}d_{1}=c_{2}c_{1}
. We thus have that
\bigcup\{p(x,d_{i}):i<\omega\}
is inconsistent. So
b_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 8.00134pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 3.92064pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 12854\relax$\kern 2.00034pt\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c_{2}
. Since also
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{u}b_{2}
and thus
c_{2}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{iLs}b_{2}
we see that weak symmetry fails and this concludes our proof.
∎

7.
Independence theorem

We recall the following facts. The first is the same as
[
KR20
, Lemma 7.4]
and the second is the same as the claim in
[
DKR19
, Lemma 5.3]
. Their proofs work in our setting as well.

Fact 7.1
.

The following hold in any thick NSOP
1
theory.

(i)

If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{d}bc
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
then
ab\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
.

(ii)

If
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
then there is
c^{\prime}
with
ac^{\prime}\equiv_{M}ac
such that
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
.

For the following lemma we borrow a trick from
[
DKR19
, Lemma 5.4]
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
T
be thick NSOP
1
and let
a\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
and
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
. Then there is
c^{\prime}
such that
ac^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}c
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
c^{*}
be such that
ac^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}c
, so
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c^{*}
. Let
N^{\prime}\supseteq M
be
\lambda_{T}
-saturated and let
q
be a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(N^{\prime}/M)
. Let
N
realise
q|_{Mabc^{*}}
, so we have
N\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{iLs}abc^{*}
. By ?THM?
(i) we then have
Na\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
and
Na\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c^{*}
. So by fact ?THM?
(ii) we find
c^{\prime}
with
Nac^{\prime}\equiv_{M}Nac^{*}
and
Na\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
. We thus have
ac^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}ac^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}c
, as required.
∎

Definition 7.3
.

We write
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
to mean that
\Lstp(b/Mc)
extends to a global
M
-Ls-invariant type
\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
for some
\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
-saturated model
N\supseteq M
. Extending
\Lstp(b/Mc)
here means that there is some
\beta\in N
with
\beta\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}b
.

The point of the enormous cardinal
\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
is that we will want to find a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model
M^{\prime}
containing
M
and a copy of
b
in
N
, and then again some
\lambda_{T}
-saturated
M^{\prime\prime}\supseteq M^{\prime}
inside
N
. By ?THM?
we can choose these
\lambda_{T}
-saturated models small enough so that this process can be repeated any finite number of times.

We easily see that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{*}
is invariant under automorphisms and, assuming thickness, that
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}M
for all
M
.

Lemma 7.4
.

We have that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{*}
satisfies the following extension properties.

(i)

(left extension) If
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
and
|d|<\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
, then there is
d^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}d
such that
bd^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
.

(ii)

(right extension) If
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
and
|d|<\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
, then there is
d^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}d
such that
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}cd^{\prime}
.

Proof.

In both cases we assume
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
. So let
q=\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
be a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(b/Mc)
for some
\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
-saturated
N\supseteq M
.

We first prove left extension. Let
N^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}N
be in
\mathfrak{M}
. By moving things by a Lascar strong automorphism over
Mc
we may assume
b\in N^{\prime}
. By ?THM?
there is
Mb\subseteq M^{\prime}\subseteq N^{\prime}
where
M^{\prime}
is
\lambda_{T}
-saturated and of cardinality
\leq 2^{\lambda_{T}+|Mb|}
. Let
d^{\prime}
realise
\tp(d/M^{\prime})
in
N^{\prime}
. So
d^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}d
while
q
also extends
\Lstp(bd^{\prime}/Mc)
, so indeed
bd^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
.

Now we prove right extension. Let
\beta\in N
be such that
\beta\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}b
. Pick
b^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}
such that
b^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mcd}\beta
. Then clearly
b^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}cd
. We finish the proof by picking
d^{\prime}
such that
bd^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}b^{\prime}d
.
∎

Proposition 7.5

(Weak independence theorem)
.

Let
T
be thick NSOP
1
. Suppose that
a\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
,
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
. Then there is
a^{\prime\prime}
with
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}a
and
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}a^{\prime}
such that
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc
.

Proof.

We may assume that
b
and
c
both enumerate a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model containing
M
. If this is not the case let
N\supseteq Mb
be
\lambda_{T}
-saturated and such that
|N|<\beth_{\omega}(\lambda_{T}+|Mbc|)
. By left extension from ?THM?
we then find
N^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}N
with
N^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
. By ?THM?
we find
a_{0}
with
a_{0}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}a
and
a_{0}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}N^{\prime}
. Now we can replace
a
by
a_{0}
and
b
by
N^{\prime}
and continue the proof. The case for
c
is analogous.

By ?THM?
there is
c^{\prime}
such that
ac^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}c
and
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
. Apply left extension from ?THM?
to
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c
and
c^{\prime}
to find
c^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}c
with
bc^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c^{\prime\prime}
. Let
b^{*}
be such that
b^{*}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc^{\prime}
and apply right extension from ?THM?
to
bc^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c^{\prime\prime}
and
b^{*}
to find
b^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc^{\prime\prime}}b^{*}
with
bc^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}b^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}
. In particular we have
b^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc^{\prime}
and
\Lstp(bc^{\prime}/Mb^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime})
extends to a global
M
-Ls-invariant type
q
. So there is a Morley sequence
(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\omega}
in
q
with
(b_{0},c_{0})=(b^{\prime\prime},c^{\prime\prime})
and
(b_{1},c_{1})=(b,c^{\prime})
. As
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc^{\prime}
, we can find
a^{*}
with
a^{*}b^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M}abc^{\prime}
such that
(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\omega}
is
Ma^{*}
-indiscernible. By construction we had
c^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}c
, so there is a Lascar strong automorphism
\sigma
over
Mb
such that
\sigma(c^{\prime\prime})=c
. Set
a^{\prime\prime}=\sigma(a^{*})
, we check that this is indeed the
a^{\prime\prime}
we are looking for.

By the chain condition (?THM?
) we have
a^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\omega}
, so we have
a^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}^{K}_{M}bc^{\prime\prime}
and
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc
then follows by invariance. By
Ma^{*}
-indiscernibility we have
a^{\prime\prime}b\equiv_{M}a^{*}b\equiv_{M}a^{*}b^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M}ab
. We assumed
b
to enumerate a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model, so indeed
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}a
. By construction of
c^{\prime}
we have
a^{\prime\prime}c\equiv_{M}a^{*}c^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{M}ac^{\prime}\equiv_{M}a^{\prime}c
. We assumed
c
to enumerate a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model, so indeed
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}a^{\prime}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Fact 7.6
.

In a thick theory, if
N\supseteq M
is
(2^{|M|+\lambda_{T}})^{+}
-saturated and
q
and
r
are global
M
-Ls-invariant types with
q|_{N}=r|_{N}
then
q=r
.

Proof.

By ?THM?
there is
M\subseteq M^{\prime}\subseteq N
where
M^{\prime}
is a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model and
|M^{\prime}|<(2^{|M|+\lambda_{T}})^{+}
. Let
\varphi(x,b)
be any formula with parameters
b
. Let
b^{\prime}\in N
realise
\tp(b/M^{\prime})
. Then
b\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b^{\prime}
. By
M
-Ls-invariance and
q|_{N}=r|_{N}
we have

\varphi(x,b)\in q\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\varphi(x,b^{\prime})\in q\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\varphi(x,b^{\prime})\in r\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\varphi(x,b)\in r,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 7.7

(Independence theorem)
.

Let
T
be a thick NSOP
1
theory. Suppose that
a\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}a^{\prime}
,
a\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b
,
a^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
and
b\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
. Then there is
a^{\prime\prime}
with
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}a
,
a^{\prime\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mc}a^{\prime}
and
a^{\prime\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}bc
.

Proof.

We may assume that
b
and
c
both enumerate a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model containing
M
. If this is not the case let
N\supseteq Mb
be
\lambda_{T}
-saturated. By extension (?THM?
) and symmetry then find
N^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}N
with
N^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}c
. Applying extension again we find
a_{0}
with
a_{0}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb}a
and
a_{0}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}N^{\prime}
. Now we can replace
a
by
a_{0}
and
b
by
N^{\prime}
and continue the proof. The case for
c
is analogous.

Let
N_{0}\supseteq M
be
(2^{|M|+\lambda_{T}})^{+}
-saturated and let
\kappa
be a big enough cardinal (depending only on
|N_{0}bc|
). Pick some global
M
-Ls-invariant type
q(y,z)
extending
\Lstp(bc/M)
such that
q
also extends to a global
M
-Ls-invariant type
\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
for some saturated enough
N\supseteq M
(depending only on
\kappa
). So there is
\beta
realising
q|_{y}
with
\beta\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b
. Let
(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\kappa}
be a Morley sequence in
q
with
b_{0}=b
and let
b_{\kappa}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\kappa}}\beta
. Then we have
b_{i}c_{i}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}b_{<i}c_{<i}
for all
i<\kappa
and
b_{\kappa}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\kappa}
.

We will inductively construct a sequence
(b_{i}^{\prime})_{i\leq\kappa}
with
b_{0}^{\prime}=b
such that at step
i
:

(i)

c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{\leq i}^{\prime}
,

(ii)

cb_{i}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}cb
,

(iii)

b_{\leq i}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\leq i}
.

The base case is already fixed:
b_{0}^{\prime}=b
. So suppose we have constructed
b_{\leq i}^{\prime}
. By induction hypothesis (iii) we can find
b^{*}b_{\leq i}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{i+1}b_{\leq i}
. So
b^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}b_{\leq i}^{\prime}
. Let
c^{*}
be such that
c^{*}b^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}cb
, so
c^{*}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b^{*}
. So also using (i) from the induction hypothesis we can apply the weak independence theorem (?THM?
) to find
c^{\prime}
such that
c^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{\leq i}^{\prime}b^{*}
,
c^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb^{*}}c^{*}
and
c^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb_{\leq i}^{\prime}}c
. We now pick
b_{i+1}^{\prime}
to be such that
cb_{i+1}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{Mb_{\leq i}^{\prime}}c^{\prime}b^{*}
. Then indeed
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{\leq i+1}^{\prime}
. We also have
b_{\leq i}^{\prime}b_{i+1}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\leq i}^{\prime}b^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\leq i}b_{i+1}
. Finally,
cb_{i+1}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c^{\prime}b^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}c^{*}b^{*}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}cb
. So this concludes the successor step. For the limit stage we assume we have constructed
b_{<i}^{\prime}
. We then have
c\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{K}b_{<i}^{\prime}
by finite character. We also have
b_{\leq j}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\leq j}
for all
j<i
. So we have
b_{<i}^{\prime}\equiv_{M}b_{<i}
. We assumed
b
to enumerate a
\lambda_{T}
-saturated model containing
M
, so because
b_{0}^{\prime}=b=b_{0}
we do in fact have
b_{<i}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{<i}
. We then construct
b_{i}^{\prime}
in an analogous way to the successor step.

We let
(c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\kappa}
be such that
b_{\kappa}^{\prime}(b_{i}^{\prime}c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\kappa}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\kappa}(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\kappa}
. So by
M
-Ls-invariance of
q|_{y}
we have
\beta b_{\kappa}^{\prime}(b_{i}^{\prime}c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\kappa}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}\beta b_{\kappa}(b_{i}c_{i})_{i<\kappa}
and thus by how we chose
b_{\kappa}
we have
b_{\kappa}^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M(b_{i}^{\prime}c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\kappa}}\beta
.

Since
q\subseteq\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
for some saturated enough
N
we can find
\beta\gamma(\beta_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i<\kappa}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b_{\kappa}^{\prime}c(b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\kappa}
in
N
, where
\beta\gamma\models q
. Here we used the fact that
b_{\kappa}^{\prime}c\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc
. Set
q^{\prime}((y_{i},z_{i})_{i<\kappa},y,z)=\tp((\beta_{i},\gamma_{i})_{i<\kappa}\beta\gamma/\mathfrak{M})
. Then
q^{\prime}
is global
M
-Ls-invariant because
\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
is global
M
-Ls-invariant. By ?THM?
and our choice of
\kappa
we get that some global
M
-Ls-invariant type
q^{\prime}|_{y_{i}z_{i}yz}
occurs for
\kappa
many
i
(modulo identifying the the variables for different
i
’s). We now focus on a subsequence of length
\omega
such that (after relabelling)
q^{\prime}|_{y_{i}z_{i}yz}
does not depend on
i
, and we forget about
\kappa
. We also relabel
b_{\kappa}^{\prime}
to
b^{\prime}
.

Claim 1.
In summary, we have just constructed the following.

(i)

A Morley sequence
(b_{i}^{\prime}c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\omega}
in
q
, where
q
is a global
M
-Ls-invariant extension of
\Lstp(bc/M)
.

(ii)

For every
i<\omega
we have
b_{i}^{\prime}c\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}b^{\prime}c\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M}bc
.

(iii)

Let
\beta\models q|_{y}
then
b^{\prime}\equiv^{\textup{Ls}}_{M(b_{i}^{\prime}c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\omega}}\beta
.

(iv)

q(y,z)\subseteq q^{\prime}((y_{i},z_{i})_{i<\omega},y,z)
and
q^{\prime}
is global
M
-Ls-invariant and extends
\Lstp((b_{i}^{\prime},c_{i}^{\prime})_{i<\omega}b^{\prime}c/M)
.

(v)

There is some sufficiently saturated
N
such that
q^{\prime}\subseteq\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
and
\tp(N/\mathfrak{M})
is
M
-Ls-invariant.

(vi)

The type
q^{\prime}|_{y_{i}z_{i}yz}
does not depend on
i
, modulo identifying variables for different
i
’s.

Claim 2.
For every
k<\omega
there are
g_{0}h_{0}g_{1}h_{1}\ldots g_{k-1}h_{k-1}g_{k}
,
g_{0}^{\prime}h_{0}^{\prime}g_{1}^{\prime}h_{1}^{\prime}\ldots g_{k-1}^{\prime}h_{k-1}^{\prime}
and
h_{0}^{\prime\prime}g_{1}^{\prime\prime}h_{1}^{\prime\prime}\ldots g_{k-1}^{\prime\prime}h_{k-1}^{\prime\prime}g_{k}^{\prime\prime}
such that:

(i)

(g_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime})_{i<k}\models(q^{\prime}|_{y_{0},z})^{\otimes k}|_{M}
,

(ii)

(h_{i}^{\prime\prime}g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime})_{i<k}\models(q^{\prime}|_{z_{0},y})^{\otimes k}|_{M}
,

(iii)

(g_{i}h_{i},g_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime})
starts an
Mg_{>i}h_{>i}g_{>i}^{\prime}h_{>i}^{\prime}
-indiscernible sequence for every
i<k
,

(iv)

(h_{i}g_{i+1},h_{i}^{\prime\prime}g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime})
starts an
Mh_{>i}g_{>i+1}h_{>i}^{\prime\prime}g_{>i+1}^{\prime\prime}
-indiscernible sequence for every
i<k
.

We first prove that the theorem follows from Claim 2. We set
p_{0}(x,y)=\tp(ab/M)
and
p_{1}(x,z)=\tp(a^{\prime}c/M)
. We will prove that
p_{0}(x,b)\cup p_{1}(x,c)
does not Kim-divide over
M
. This is enough, because by ?THM?
we can then extend it to a complete type that does not Kim-divide over
M
. Since we assumed
b
and
c
to enumerate
\lambda_{T}
-saturated models containing
M
, any realisation
a^{\prime\prime}
of that complete type is then what we needed to construct.

By compactness we can find
M
-indiscernible
(g_{i}h_{i}g_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}g_{i}^{\prime\prime}h_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
(g_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\models(q^{\prime}|_{y_{0},z})^{\otimes\mathbb{Z}}|_{M}
and
(h_{i}^{\prime\prime}g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\models(q^{\prime}|_{z_{0},y})^{\otimes\mathbb{Z}}|_{M}
. Furthermore, we can make it so that for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have
g_{i}h_{i}\equiv_{Mg_{>i}h_{>i}g_{>i}^{\prime}h_{>i}^{\prime}}g_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}
and
h_{i}g_{i+1}\equiv_{Mh_{>i}g_{>i+1}h_{>i}^{\prime\prime}g_{>i+1}^{\prime\prime}}h_{i}^{\prime\prime}g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime}
. We have that
q^{\prime}|_{y,z_{0}}\supseteq\tp(b^{\prime}c_{0}^{\prime}/M)
, by Claim 1(iv). So by Claim 1(iii) and (v) we have that
b^{\prime}\mathop{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\textstyle\kern 5.71527pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 3.87495pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 5.71527pt}{\scriptstyle\kern 2.80048pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 1.89871pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 2.80048pt}{\scriptscriptstyle\kern 1.42882pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mid$\hss}\lower 0.96873pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\smile$\hss}\kern 1.42882pt}}_{M}^{*}c_{0}^{\prime}
. Then by ?THM?
we have that
p_{0}(x,g_{1}^{\prime\prime})\cup p_{1}(x,h_{0}^{\prime\prime})
does not Kim-divide. Then because
(h_{i}^{\prime\prime}g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime})_{i\geq n}\models(q^{\prime}|_{z_{0},y})^{\otimes\omega}|_{M}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we get that
\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}p_{0}(x,g_{i+1}^{\prime\prime})\cup p_{1}(x,h_{i}^{\prime\prime})
is consistent. By the parallel sequences lemma (?THM?
) we thus have that
\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}p_{0}(x,g_{i+1})\cup p_{1}(x,h_{i})
is consistent. This is the same set as
\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}p_{0}(x,g_{i})\cup p_{1}(x,h_{i})
. So again by the parallel sequences lemma we get that
\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}p_{0}(x,g_{i}^{\prime})\cup p_{1}(x,h_{i}^{\prime})
is consistent. By Claim 1(ii) and (iii) we have that
q^{\prime}|_{y_{0},z}
extends
\Lstp(bc/M)
. So we conclude that
p_{0}(x,b)\cup p_{1}(x,c)
does not Kim-divide over
M
, as required.

We are left to verify Claim 2. We fix

and by backwards induction on

we will define trees
